The system of partial differential equations for the C 0 C_{{}_{0}} function
Tai-Fu Feng a , Chao-Hsi Chang b,c,d , Jian-Bin Chen e , Hai-Bin Zhang a

Abstract
We present an approach to analyze the scalar integrals of any Feynman diagrams in detail here. This method not only completely recovers some well-known results in the literature, but also produces some new results on the C 0 C_{{}_{0}} function. The approach can be employed to evaluate the coefficient of arbitrary power of ε \varepsilon in the expansion of a scalar integral, where D = 4 − 2 ​ ε D=4-2\varepsilon denotes the time-space dimension.



pacs
02.30.Jr, 11.10.Gh, 12.38.Bx

I
Introduction

The discovery of the Higgs particle in the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) implies
the great success of the standard model (SM)
CMS2012
;
ATLAS2012
.
With the increasing of luminosity of the collider, one of the targets
for particle physics now is to test the SM precisely and to search for
new physics (NP) beyond the SM
CEPC-SPPC
;
ILC
;
HI-LHC
.

How to evaluate the scalar integrals exactly is an open problem to predict
the electroweak observables precisely in the SM. The author of literature
V.A.Smirnov2012

presents several methods to evaluate those scalar integrals.
Nevertheless each method mentioned in this literature
has its blemishes since it can only be applied to the Feynman diagrams with
special topology and kinematic invariants.

In dimensional regularization, any scalar integral can be expanded
around
\varepsilon=0
, here the time-space dimension
D=4-2\varepsilon
.
For example, the one-loop three-point function
C_{{}_{0}}
is expanded as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}=C_{{}_{0}}^{(0)}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}C_{{}_{0}}^{(i)}\varepsilon^{i}\;,

(1)

around
D=4
, and the well-known result of
C_{{}_{0}}^{(0)}
is presented in the
literature
tHooft1979
. However, evaluating
C_{{}_{0}}^{(i)}

precisely is also necessary to obtain high order radiative corrections
to the amplitude self-consistently when the virtual interactions
originate from the counter terms
Collins1984
.
In addition, the vector and tensor coefficients
C_{{}_{i}}
,
C_{{}_{00}}
,

C_{{}_{ij}}
,
(i,\;j=1,\;2)
adopted in the paper
Passarino1979

can be expressed by the one-point function

A_{{}_{0}}
, the two-point function
B_{{}_{0}}
, and the three-point function
C_{{}_{0}}
,
respectively.

There are many analytical results on the scalar integral
C_{{}_{0}}
already
in the references. In Ref.
Davydychev1
the massless
C_{{}_{0}}
function
is presented as the linear combination of the fourth kind Appell function

F_{{}_{4}}
whose arguments are the dimensionless ratios among the external momenta squared,
and is simplified further as the linear combination of the Gauss function
{}_{2}F_{1}

through the quadratic transformation
Davydychev1993NPB
in the literature
Davydychev2000
.
With some special assumptions on the virtual masses,
the analytic expressions of the scalar integral
C_{{}_{0}}
are given by
the multiple hypergeometric functions in Ref.
Davydychev3
through the corresponding
Mellin-Barnes representations. Taking the massless
C_{{}_{0}}
function as
an example, the author of Ref
Davydychev1992JPA
presents an algorithm
to evaluate the scalar integrals of one-loop vertex-type Feynman diagrams.
Certainly, some analytic results of the
C_{{}_{0}}
function can also be extracted from
the expressions for the scalar integrals of one-loop massive
N-
point
Feynman diagrams
Davydychev1991JMP
;
Davydychev1992JMP
. In addition,
the literature
Davydychev2006
also provides a geometrical interpretation
of the analytic expressions of the scalar integrals from one-loop
N-
point
Feynman diagrams. Using the recurrence relations respecting the time-space
dimension, the papers
Tarasov2000
;
Tarasov2003
formulate one-loop
two-point function
B_{{}_{0}}
as the linear combination of the Gauss function

{}_{2}F_{1}
, one-loop three-point function
C_{{}_{0}}

with arbitrary external momenta and virtual masses as the linear combination
of the Appell function
F_{{}_{1}}
with two arguments,
and one-loop four-point function
D_{{}_{0}}
with arbitrary external momenta
and virtual masses as the linear combination of the Lauricella-Saran function
F_{{}_{s}}

with three arguments, respectively. The expression for the scalar integral
C_{{}_{0}}
is convenient
for analytic continuation and numerical evaluation because continuation of the Appell
functions has been analyzed thoroughly. Nevertheless, how to perform continuation
of the Lauricella-Saran function
F_{{}_{s}}
outside its convergent domain is still a challenge.
Basing on the hypergeometric system of linear partial differential equations (PDEs),
we present an approach to evaluate the scalar integral of arbitrary multiloop Feynman
diagram systematically. Actually the system of PDEs satisfied by the
corresponding scalar integral is a holonomic integrable system which can be transformed
into the Pfaffian system of PDEs
M.E.Taylor12
by the algebra decomposition.
Then one can obtain the singularities of the holonomic hypergeometric system
and the number of independent solutions in certain parameter space.
Together with the analytic expressions in some convergent regions,
the holonomic hypergeometric system provides a new way to understand
the corresponding scalar integral. Taking the
C_{{}_{0}}
function as an example, we elucidate
how to evaluate the coefficient of arbitrary power of
\varepsilon
in the expansion of
a scalar integral around
D=4
. In fact, the corresponding analytic results
on the
C_{{}_{0}}
function coincide with those well-known results mentioned above.
The evaluations of scalar integrals of any Feynman diagrams such as that presented in
Refs.
Davydychev1993
;
V.A.Smirnov1999
;
J.B.Tausk1999
are given elsewhere.

A holonomic hypergeometric system of linear PDEs
is given through the corresponding Mellin-Barnes representation of the concerned
scalar integral
M.Y.Kalmykov12
, where the system of linear PDEs is satisfied by the scalar integral
in the whole parameter space of the independent variables.
The scalar integral is written as the multiple hypergeometric
functions
L.J.Slater66
of the independent variables for some isolated singularities
of the integrand by residue theorem
J.Leray1959
.
In addition, the Horn’s study of convergence
Horn1889
predicts the
absolutely and uniformly convergent regions of those derived hypergeometric functions
exactly. Several convergent regions of the hypergeometric functions compose a set in which
each convergent region does not intersect with the others. Additionally
each convergent region of the set either does not intersect with,
or is a proper subset of other convergent regions which do not belong to the set.
Within each convergent region in the set, the scalar
integral can be written as the sum of those hypergeometric functions
whose convergent regions contain the concerned element entirely.

In view of mathematics, the most important point for a given multiple power series is how to
get its absolutely and uniformly convergent region, and how to continue
it to the whole parameter space.
Fortunately, the continuation can be achieved by the system of linear PDEs mentioned above.
As stated in our previous work
Feng2018
,
an effective Hamiltonian can be constructed if those linear PDEs are
the stationary conditions of the corresponding system. Then
we can numerically continue the scalar integral to the entire parameter space
with the finite element method.

Our presentation is organized as follows. We briefly mention some typical results
of the massive
C_{{}_{0}}
function in section
II
at first. Then we present in detail
our analyses on the massless
C_{{}_{0}}
function in section
III
,
the
C_{{}_{0}}
function with one nonzero virtual mass in section
IV
,
and the
C_{{}_{0}}
function with three equally virtual masses in section
V
, respectively.
In section
VI
, we recognize the system of linear PDEs as the
stationary conditions of a functional under some restrictions according to
Hamilton’s principle, which is convenient to numerically continue
the scalar integral to the whole parameter space with the finite element method.
The conclusions are summarized in section
VII
and some tedious formulae are presented in the appendixes.

II
Some results of the
C_{{}_{0}}
function in general case

The Mellin-Barnes representation of massive
C_{{}_{0}}
function is generally written as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{3}}^{2})

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=\int{d^{D}q\over(2\pi)^{D}}{1\over(q^{2}-m_{{}_{3}}^{2})((q+p_{{}_{1}})^{2}-m_{{}_{2}}^{2})((q-p_{{}_{2}})^{2}-m_{{}_{1}}^{2})}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt=-{i(-)^{D/2-3}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}(2\pi i)^{5}}\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}ds_{{}_{1}}\Big(-{m_{{}_{1}}^{2}\over p_{{}_{3}}^{2}}\Big)^{s_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma(-s_{{}_{1}})\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}ds_{{}_{2}}\Big(-{m_{{}_{2}}^{2}\over p_{{}_{3}}^{2}}\Big)^{s_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(-s_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\times\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}ds_{{}_{3}}\Big(-{m_{{}_{3}}^{2}\over p_{{}_{3}}^{2}}\Big)^{s_{{}_{3}}}\Gamma(-s_{{}_{3}})\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}dz_{{}_{1}}\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}dz_{{}_{2}}\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\times\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+s_{{}_{1}}+s_{{}_{2}}+s_{{}_{3}}+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})\Big({p_{{}_{1}}^{2}\over p_{{}_{3}}^{2}}\Big)^{z_{{}_{1}}}\Big({p_{{}_{2}}^{2}\over p_{{}_{3}}^{2}}\Big)^{z_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\times{\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s_{{}_{1}}-s_{{}_{3}}-z_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s_{{}_{2}}-s_{{}_{3}}-z_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(1+s_{{}_{3}}+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(D-3-s_{{}_{1}}-s_{{}_{2}}-s_{{}_{3}})}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt={i(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}F_{{}_{p_{{}_{3}}}}(\xi_{{}_{13}},\;\xi_{{}_{23}},\;\xi_{{}_{33}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}})

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt={i(m_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}F_{{}_{m_{{}_{3}}}}(\eta_{{}_{13}},\;\eta_{{}_{23}},\;\eta_{{}_{33}},\;y_{{}_{13}},\;y_{{}_{23}})

(2)

with
p_{{}_{3}}^{2}=(p_{{}_{1}}+p_{{}_{2}})^{2}
,
\xi_{{}_{ij}}=-m_{{}_{i}}^{2}/p_{{}_{j}}^{2}
,

\eta_{{}_{ij}}=-p_{{}_{i}}^{2}/m_{{}_{j}}^{2}=1/\xi_{{}_{ji}}
,
x_{{}_{ij}}=p_{{}_{i}}^{2}/p_{{}_{j}}^{2}
,
and
y_{{}_{ij}}=m_{{}_{i}}^{2}/m_{{}_{j}}^{2}\;(i,\;j=1,2,3)
, respectively.
Here the dimensionless functions
F_{{}_{p_{{}_{3}}}}
,
\xi_{{}_{33}}^{D/2-3}F_{{}_{m_{{}_{3}}}}

comply with the holonomic hypergeometric system of linear PDEs

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big[3-{D\over 2}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{i3}}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]\Big[1+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]\Big[4-D+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-{1\over\xi_{{}_{33}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{3}\Big[2-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{i3}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x_{{}_{(3-i)3}}}}\Big]\Big\}F_{{}_{p_{{}_{3}}}}=0\;,

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big[3-{D\over 2}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{i3}}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]\Big[4-D+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+{1\over\xi_{{}_{j3}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{j3}}}}\Big[2-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{j3}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x_{{}_{(3-j)3}}}}\Big]\Big\}F_{{}_{p_{{}_{3}}}}=0\;,

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big[3-{D\over 2}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{i3}}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]\Big[1+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-{1\over x_{{}_{j3}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x_{{}_{j3}}}}\Big[2-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\xi_{{}_{(3-j)3}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x_{{}_{j3}}}}\Big]\Big\}F_{{}_{p_{{}_{3}}}}=0,\;(j=1,\;2)\;,

(3)

with the Euler operator
\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{x}}=x\partial/\partial x
etc.
Or equivalently, the functions
F_{{}_{m_{{}_{3}}}}
,
\eta_{{}_{33}}^{D/2-3}F_{{}_{p_{{}_{3}}}}

satisfy the following holonomic system of linear PDEs

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big[3-{D\over 2}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{y_{{}_{i3}}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]\prod\limits_{i=1}^{2}\Big[1+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{i3}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{33}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{y_{{}_{(3-i)3}}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-{1\over\eta_{{}_{33}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{33}}}}\Big[{D\over 2}-1+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]\Big[2-{D\over 2}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{y_{{}_{i3}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{33}}}}\Big]\Big\}F_{{}_{m_{{}_{3}}}}=0\;,

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big[3-{D\over 2}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{y_{{}_{i3}}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]\Big[1+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{j3}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{33}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{y_{{}_{(3-j)3}}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt+{1\over\eta_{{}_{j3}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{j3}}}}\Big[{D\over 2}-1+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]\Big\}F_{{}_{m_{{}_{3}}}}=0\;,

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big[3-{D\over 2}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{y_{{}_{i3}}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{i3}}}}\Big]\Big[1+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{(3-j)3}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{33}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{y_{{}_{j3}}}}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-{1\over y_{{}_{j3}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{y_{{}_{j3}}}}\Big[2-{D\over 2}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{y_{{}_{i3}}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{{}_{\eta_{{}_{33}}}}\Big]\Big\}F_{{}_{m_{{}_{3}}}}=0\;,\;\;(j=1,\;2)\;.

(4)

Here we emphasize that the two systems in
Eq.(
3
) and Eq.(
4
) are equivalent.
Actually the Mellin-Barnes representation presented here is
equivalent to that of Eq. (3.4) in Ref.
Davydychev1992JMP
.
In addition, the author of Ref.
Davydychev1992JMP
also
presents a single hypergeometric function originating from the Mellin-Barnes representation
in Eq. (3.6). With our notation, the corresponding hypergeometric function is rewritten as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{m_{{}_{3}}}}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{3}}^{2})={i(m_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\sum\limits_{j_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{3}}=0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\times{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}+n_{{}_{3}}}\over j_{{}_{1}}!j_{{}_{2}}!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!n_{{}_{3}}!}\Big[1-y_{{}_{13}}\Big]^{j_{{}_{1}}}\Big[1-y_{{}_{23}}\Big]^{j_{{}_{2}}}\eta_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}\eta_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}\eta_{{}_{33}}^{n_{{}_{3}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\times{\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j_{{}_{1}}+j_{{}_{2}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}n_{{}_{i}})\Gamma(1+j_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}+n_{{}_{3}})\over\Gamma(3+j_{{}_{1}}+j_{{}_{2}}+2\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}n_{{}_{i}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 79.6678pt\times\Gamma(1+j_{{}_{2}}+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{3}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\;,

(5)

which obviously satisfies the holonomic hypergeometric systems in
Eq.(
3
) and Eq.(
4
).
Using the adjacent ratios of the coefficients, we define

\displaystyle\Phi_{{}_{j_{k}}}(j_{{}_{1}},j_{{}_{2}},n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}},n_{{}_{3}})={(j_{{}_{1}}+j_{{}_{2}}+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}+n_{{}_{3}})(j_{{}_{k}}+n_{{}_{3-k}}+n_{{}_{3}})\over j_{{}_{k}}(j_{{}_{1}}+j_{{}_{2}}+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}}+2n_{{}_{3}})}\;,

\displaystyle\Phi_{{}_{n_{k}}}(j_{{}_{1}},j_{{}_{2}},n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}},n_{{}_{3}})={(j_{{}_{1}}+j_{{}_{2}}+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}+n_{{}_{3}})(j_{{}_{3-k}}+n_{{}_{k}}+n_{{}_{3}})(n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{k}}(j_{{}_{1}}+j_{{}_{2}}+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}}+2n_{{}_{3}})^{2}}\;,

\displaystyle\Phi_{{}_{n_{3}}}(j_{{}_{1}},j_{{}_{2}},n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}},n_{{}_{3}})={(j_{{}_{1}}+j_{{}_{2}}+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}+n_{{}_{3}})\prod\limits_{i=1}^{2}(j_{{}_{3-i}}+n_{{}_{i}}+n_{{}_{3}})\over n_{{}_{3}}(j_{{}_{1}}+j_{{}_{2}}+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}}+2n_{{}_{3}})^{2}},\;(k=1,\;2).

(6)

The Cartesian hypersurface of the multiple hypergeometric
series in Eq.(
5
) is written as

\displaystyle r_{{}_{1}}^{2}r_{{}_{3}}-r_{{}_{1}}r_{{}_{2}}r_{{}_{3}}+r_{{}_{3}}^{2}-r_{{}_{1}}r_{{}_{3}}^{2}-r_{{}_{1}}r_{{}_{2}}r_{{}_{4}}+r_{{}_{2}}^{2}r_{{}_{4}}-2r_{{}_{3}}r_{{}_{4}}+r_{{}_{1}}r_{{}_{3}}r_{{}_{4}}+r_{{}_{2}}r_{{}_{3}}r_{{}_{4}}

\displaystyle+r_{{}_{4}}^{2}-r_{{}_{2}}r_{{}_{4}}^{2}+r_{{}_{1}}r_{{}_{2}}r_{{}_{5}}-2r_{{}_{3}}r_{{}_{5}}+r_{{}_{1}}r_{{}_{3}}r_{{}_{5}}-2r_{{}_{4}}r_{{}_{5}}+r_{{}_{2}}r_{{}_{4}}r_{{}_{5}}+r_{{}_{3}}r_{{}_{4}}r_{{}_{5}}+r_{{}_{5}}^{2}=0\;.

(7)

with
r_{{}_{i}}=|y_{{}_{i3}}-1|,\;r_{{}_{2+i}}=|\eta_{{}_{i3}}|,\;(i=1,\;2),\;r_{{}_{5}}=|\eta_{{}_{33}}|
, respectively.
Correspondingly the absolutely and uniformly convergent domain of the series is

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{1}}^{\prime,a}=\left\{(\eta_{{}_{13}},\;\eta_{{}_{23}},\;\eta_{{}_{33}},\;y_{{}_{13}},\;y_{{}_{23}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\eta_{{}_{13}}|<4,\;|\eta_{{}_{23}}|<4,\;|\eta_{{}_{33}}|<8,\\
|y_{{}_{13}}-1|<1,\;|y_{{}_{23}}-1|<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

Through the recurrence relations with respect to the time-space dimension
Tarasov2000
;
Tarasov2003
,
the scalar integral
C_{{}_{0}}
can also be presented by the linear combination
of the first kind Appell hypergeometric function
F_{{}_{1}}
. Where the independent
variables of the hypergeometric functions are dimensionless ratios among
the parameters
r_{{}_{12}}
,
r_{{}_{23}}
,
r_{{}_{13}}
,
r_{{}_{123}}
, and
m_{{}_{i}},\;(i=1,\;2,\;3)
,
and the linear combination coefficients depend on the parameters

\lambda_{{}_{12}}
,
\lambda_{{}_{23}}
,
\lambda_{{}_{13}}
, and
g_{{}_{123}}
.
With our notation above, those parameters are expressed as

\displaystyle\lambda_{{}_{12}}=\kappa^{2}(m_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2}),\;\;\;\lambda_{{}_{13}}=\kappa^{2}(m_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{3}}^{2}),

\displaystyle\lambda_{{}_{23}}=\kappa^{2}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;m_{{}_{3}}^{2}),\;\;\;g_{{}_{123}}=-2\kappa^{2}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2}),

\displaystyle\lambda_{{}_{123}}=8m_{{}_{1}}^{2}m_{{}_{2}}^{2}m_{{}_{3}}^{2}-2m_{{}_{1}}^{2}(m_{{}_{2}}^{2}+m_{{}_{3}}^{2}-p_{{}_{1}}^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98866pt-2m_{{}_{2}}^{2}(m_{{}_{1}}^{2}+m_{{}_{3}}^{2}-p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{2}-2m_{{}_{3}}^{2}(m_{{}_{1}}^{2}+m_{{}_{2}}^{2}-p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98866pt+2(m_{{}_{1}}^{2}+m_{{}_{2}}^{2}-p_{{}_{3}}^{2})(m_{{}_{1}}^{2}+m_{{}_{3}}^{2}-p_{{}_{2}}^{2})(m_{{}_{2}}^{2}+m_{{}_{3}}^{2}-p_{{}_{1}}^{2})\;,

\displaystyle r_{{}_{12}}=-{\lambda_{{}_{12}}\over 4p_{{}_{3}}^{2}},\;\;\;r_{{}_{13}}=-{\lambda_{{}_{13}}\over 4p_{{}_{2}}^{2}},\;\;\;r_{{}_{23}}=-{\lambda_{{}_{23}}\over 4p_{{}_{1}}^{2}},\;\;\;r_{{}_{123}}={\lambda_{{}_{123}}\over g_{{}_{123}}}\;,

(11)

with the Källèn function squared

\displaystyle\kappa^{2}(x,y,z)=2xy+2yz+2zx-x^{2}-y^{2}-z^{2}\;.

(12)

Using the preparation above, one can check that the analytic expression in Eq. (74)
of Ref.
Tarasov2003
complies with the holonomic hypergeometric systems in
Eq.(
3
).

In order to apply the residue theorem, we should choose five
\Gamma
functions
in the numerator of the integrand to determine the poles because the Mellin-Barnes
representation in Eq. (
2
) is a five-fold contour integral.
There are
C_{9}^{5}=126
different choices in all.
When the poles are determined by
some specific choices of
\Gamma
functions, for example,
\Gamma(-s_{{}_{3}})
,

\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
,
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})
,
\Gamma(1+s_{{}_{3}}+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
,
and
\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+s_{{}_{1}}+s_{{}_{2}}+s_{{}_{3}}+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
,
the Jacobian of the transformation

\displaystyle-s_{{}_{3}}^{\prime}=-s_{{}_{3}},\;

\displaystyle-s_{{}_{1}}^{\prime}=3-{D\over 2}+s_{{}_{1}}+s_{{}_{2}}+s_{{}_{3}}+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}},\;

\displaystyle-s_{{}_{2}}^{\prime}=1+s_{{}_{3}}+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}},\;

\displaystyle-z_{{}_{1}}^{\prime}=-z_{{}_{1}},\;

\displaystyle-z_{{}_{2}}^{\prime}=-z_{{}_{2}}

(13)

is zero. Correspondingly this special choice of poles in the
numerator of the integrand does not induce correction to the Feynman
integral at all
J.Leray1959
. For other choices of poles that
the Jacobian of the corresponding transformation is not zero, the Feynman integral
is written as a quintuple hypergeometric function.

In the hypergeometric function mentioned above, the coefficient of the power function of
independent variables is a fraction, whose numerator
and denominator are both products of several
\Gamma
functions.
Where the independent variable of each
\Gamma
function is a linear combination
of the time-space dimension and the summation indices, and the coefficients
of the summation indices are either positive or negative integers.
Because of this reason, the hypergeometric function is decomposed into the sum of several hypergeometric
series, so that the independent variables of all
\Gamma
functions
of each ’new’ hypergeometric series have definite signs at
D=4
.
After the transformation of summation indices, we obtain
the absolutely and uniformly convergent region of each ’new’ hypergeometric series
with Horn’s study of convergence
Horn1889
.
The intersection of all convergent regions is the absolutely and uniformly
convergent region of the original hypergeometric function.

Some convergent regions of the quintuple hypergeometric functions compose a set,
and each convergent region does not intersect with the others in the set. Additionally each convergent region
in the set either does not intersect with,
or is a proper subset of the other convergent region which does not belong to the set.
In each convergent region of the set, the Feynman
integral can be written as the sum of those hypergeometric functions
whose convergent regions contain the concerned region of the set entirely.

In order to shorten the length of text, we apply the approach to
the Feynman integrals of the
C_{{}_{0}}
function with some special assumptions
on the virtual masses.

III
The scalar integral of massless
C_{{}_{0}}
function

Generally the Mellin-Barnes representation of massless
C_{{}_{0}}
function is
simplified as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2})=\int{d^{D}q\over(2\pi)^{D}}{1\over q^{2}(q+p_{{}_{1}})^{2}(q-p_{{}_{2}})^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 73.97733pt={i(-)^{D/2}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}\Gamma(D-3)(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}dz_{{}_{1}}\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}dz_{{}_{2}}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2})^{z_{{}_{1}}}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{z_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt\times(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3-z_{{}_{1}}-z_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt\times\Gamma(1+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-z_{{}_{1}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-z_{{}_{2}})\;,

(14)

which coincides with Eq. (24) of Ref.
Davydychev3
(i.e. Eq. (2.5)
of Ref.
Davydychev1992JPA
). The analytic expression of the
massless
C_{{}_{0}}
function is obtained by summing over residues of the integrand,
and the corresponding results are enumerated as following.

\bullet
1(a):

After summing over the residues of
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
and
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})
,
we obtain the double hypergeometric function as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,1a}}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2})={i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}\Gamma(D-3)}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}\over n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}\Big({p_{{}_{1}}^{2}\over p_{{}_{3}}^{2}}\Big)^{n_{{}_{1}}}\Big({p_{{}_{2}}^{2}\over p_{{}_{3}}^{2}}\Big)^{n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt\times\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-n_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt\times\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-n_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 76.82234pt={i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt\times A_{{}_{n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(1a)}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}\;.

(15)

The necessary condition
|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>\max(|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|)

(i.e.
|x_{{}_{i3}}|<1
) should be satisfied to guarantee the uniform absolute-convergence
of the double series. Here the adjacent ratios of the coefficients are

\displaystyle\Phi_{{}_{1}}^{\prime}(n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}})={A_{{}_{1+n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(1a)}\over A_{{}_{n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(1a)}}={(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})(3-D/2+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over(1+n_{{}_{1}})(3-D/2+n_{{}_{1}})}\;,

\displaystyle\Phi_{{}_{2}}^{\prime}(n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}})={A_{{}_{n_{{}_{1}},1+n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(1a)}\over A_{{}_{n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(1a)}}={(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})(3-D/2+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over(1+n_{{}_{2}})(3-D/2+n_{{}_{2}})}\;.

\displaystyle\Phi_{{}_{2}}(n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}})=\lim\limits_{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}\Phi_{{}_{2}}^{\prime}(\lambda n_{{}_{1}},\lambda n_{{}_{2}})={(n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})^{2}\over n_{{}_{2}}^{2}}\;.

IV
The
C_{{}_{0}}
function with one nonzero virtual mass

In this case, the Mellin-Barnes representation of the scalar integral is

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=\int{d^{D}q\over(2\pi)^{D}}{1\over(q^{2}-m^{2})(q+p_{{}_{1}})^{2}(q-p_{{}_{2}})^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt=-{i(-)^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}(2\pi i)^{3}}\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}ds(-m^{2})^{s}\Gamma(-s)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}dz_{{}_{1}}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2})^{z_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}dz_{{}_{2}}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{z_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3-s-z_{{}_{1}}-z_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+s+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s-z_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s-z_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(1+s+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(D-3-s)}\;.

(51)

which coincides with Eq. (26) of Ref.
Davydychev3
(i.e. Eq. (4.3)
of Ref.
Davydychev1992JMP
).
Applying the residue theorem to the singularities
of
\Gamma
functions in the numerator of integrand in the Mellin-Barnes
representation, we formulate the scalar integral
as various hypergeometric functions whose absolutely and uniformly
convergent regions are analyzed through the Horn’s study of convergence
Horn1889
.
If the scalar integral is expressed as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})={i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}F_{{}_{a,p_{{}_{3}}}}(\xi_{{}_{33}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}})

(52)

with
\xi_{{}_{33}}=-m^{2}/p_{{}_{3}}^{2}
, then the dimensionless function
F_{{}_{a,p_{{}_{3}}}}
satisfies the
holonomic hypergeometric system of linear PDEs

\displaystyle\Big\{(3-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{i3}}})(1+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{i3}}})(4-D+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}})

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-{1\over\xi_{{}_{33}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{2}(2-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{i3}}})\Big\}F_{{}_{a,p_{{}_{3}}}}=0\;,

\displaystyle\Big\{(3-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{i3}}})(1+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{i3}}})

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-{1\over x_{{}_{j3}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{j3}}}(2-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{33}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{j3}}})\Big\}F_{{}_{a,p_{{}_{3}}}}=0\;,\;\;(j=1,\;2)\;.

(53)

Setting
m_{{}_{1}}=m_{{}_{2}}=0
in the corresponding system of linear PDEs of Eq. (
3
),
we find the first, fourth and fifth PDEs composing the system presented above.
Now we present our results in detail below.

IV.1
The hypergeometric functions and their convergent regions

\bullet
1:

For the isolated singularities defined by the poles of

\Gamma(-s)
,
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
, and
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})
in the numerator of integrand
of the Mellin-Barnes representation, the derived hypergeometric function is written as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,1}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})={i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)\Gamma(4-D)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}{\Gamma(4-D+j)\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 105.2751pt={i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt+{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)\Gamma(4-D)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}{\Gamma(4-D+j)\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{2}})}\;.

(54)

The condition
|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>\max(m^{2},\;|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|)
is necessary
to guarantee the convergence of the hypergeometric series. In addition,

D=4
is the second order pole of the first double hypergeometric
function, and the first order pole of the second triple hypergeometric
function, respectively. Certainly the expression
of Eq. (
54
) complies with the system of the PDEs in Eq. (
53
) explicitly.

The absolutely and uniformly convergent region of the hypergeometric functions
is given by

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{1}}^{a}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{33}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}\sqrt{|x_{{}_{13}}|}+\sqrt{|x_{{}_{23}}|}<1,\;|\xi_{{}_{33}}|+|x_{{}_{13}}|<1,\\
|\xi_{{}_{33}}|+|x_{{}_{23}}|<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

\bullet
2:

For the isolated singularities defined by the poles of

\Gamma(-s)
,
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
, and
\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s-z_{{}_{2}})
in the numerator of integrand
of the Mellin-Barnes representation, the corresponding hypergeometric series is given as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,2}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)\Gamma(4-D)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{D/2-2-j+n_{{}_{2}}}{\Gamma(4-D+j)\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1-j+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 105.2751pt=-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=1}^{\infty}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{D/2-2+n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)\Gamma(4-D)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=1}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{D/2-2+n_{{}_{2}}}{\Gamma(4-D+j)\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!(j+n_{{}_{2}})!}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)\Gamma(4-D)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{j+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{D/2-2-j}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{(-)^{j}\Gamma(4-D+j+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(2-{D\over 2}+j)\over(j+n_{{}_{2}})!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)\Gamma(4-D)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{D/2-2}{\Gamma(4-D+j)\over(j!)^{2}n_{{}_{1}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j+n_{{}_{1}})\over\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{D/2-2}{\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}})\over\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}})}\;.

(58)

The essential condition
|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>\max(m^{2},\;|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|)
,
|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>m^{2}

should be satisfied to guarantee the hypergeometric series converging.
In the above equation, one derives the first expression after applying the
residue theorem to the Mellin-Barnes representation.
The second expression decomposes the summation indices of the hypergeometric function
in the first expression, so that
D=4
is the pole of the same fixed order
for each term of every hypergeometric series in the second expression.
Furthermore, we can check the second expression satisfying the system of linear PDEs
in Eq. (
53
) explicitly, this fact implies that the decomposition of
summation indices above is correct and reasonable.

Horn’s theory of convergence predicts the absolutely and uniformly convergent
region of the hypergeometric functions as

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{2}}^{a}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{33}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\xi_{{}_{33}}|<|x_{{}_{23}}|,\;\sqrt{|x_{{}_{13}}|}+\sqrt{|x_{{}_{23}}|}<1,\\
|\xi_{{}_{33}}|+|x_{{}_{13}}|<1,\;|\xi_{{}_{33}}|+|x_{{}_{23}}|<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

3:

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,3}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)\Gamma(4-D)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{-1-j-n_{{}_{1}}-n_{{}_{2}}}{(-)^{j}\Gamma(4-D+j)\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 105.2751pt=-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}x_{{}_{12}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2+n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)\Gamma(4-D)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{j}x_{{}_{12}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2+n_{{}_{2}}}{(-)^{j}\Gamma(4-D+j)\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{2}})}\;.

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\xi_{{}_{32}}^{j}x_{{}_{12}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{32}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}{(-)^{j}\Gamma(4-D+j)\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{18}}^{a}=\left\{(\eta_{{}_{13}},\;\eta_{{}_{23}},\;\eta_{{}_{33}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\eta_{{}_{13}}|+|\eta_{{}_{33}}|<1,\;|\eta_{{}_{23}}|+|\eta_{{}_{33}}|<1,\\
|\eta_{{}_{33}}|<(1-|\eta_{{}_{13}}|)(1-|\eta_{{}_{23}}|)\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

Certainly the scalar integral
of Eq. (
91
) also satisfies the system of PDEs in Eq. (
53
).

\bullet

,

, and

,

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,19}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})={i(m^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Gamma(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}\eta_{{}_{33}}^{j}\eta_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}\eta_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma({D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j)}\;.

(95)

) totally coincides with

\Omega_{{}_{18}}^{a}
in Eq.(

).

\bullet

,

, and

,

|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>m^{2}
,

should be satisfied

\bullet
23:

For the isolated singularities defined by the poles of

\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
,
\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s-z_{{}_{1}})
, and
\Gamma(1+s+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
,
the corresponding hypergeometric series is

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,23}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})={i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Gamma(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{D/2-2+j-n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{1-D/2-j-n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{(-)^{j+n_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(2-{D\over 2}-j+n_{{}_{1}})\over\Gamma({D\over 2}-1-j+n_{{}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 105.2751pt={i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Gamma(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2-n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{12}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2+n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\over j!(j+n_{{}_{1}})!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(2-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}})\over\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt+{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Gamma(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2+j}x_{{}_{12}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2+n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{(-)^{j}\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over(j+n_{{}_{1}})!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(2-{D\over 2}+j)\over\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt+{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2}x_{{}_{12}}^{j}x_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2+n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\over(j!)^{2}n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{2}})}\;.

(96)

The condition
|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>\max(|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|,\;m^{2})
,

m^{2}>|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|
is essential to guarantee the convergence of the hypergeometric series.
By implementing the residue theorem, one derives the first expression above.
The second expression decomposes the summation indices of the hypergeometric series
in the first expression, so that
D=4
is the pole of the same fixed order
for each term of every hypergeometric series in the second expression above.
In addition, the second expression also satisfies the system of linear PDEs
in Eq. (
53
).

Correspondingly the convergent region of the series is given by

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{23}}^{a}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{32}},\;x_{{}_{12}},\;x_{{}_{32}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|x_{{}_{12}}|<|\xi_{{}_{32}}|,\;\sqrt{|x_{{}_{12}}|}+\sqrt{|x_{{}_{32}}|}<1,\\
|\xi_{{}_{32}}|+|x_{{}_{32}}|<1,\;|\xi_{{}_{32}}|+|x_{{}_{12}}|<1,\\
|x_{{}_{12}}|+|\xi_{{}_{32}}x_{{}_{32}}|<|\xi_{{}_{32}}|,\;|x_{{}_{12}}|(1+|\xi_{{}_{32}}|)<|\xi_{{}_{32}}|\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

\bullet
24:

For the isolated singularities defined by the poles of

\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
,
\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s-z_{{}_{1}})
, and

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,24}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Gamma(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2-n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{12}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{32}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\over j!(j+n_{{}_{1}})!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(2-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}})\over\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+n_{{}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Gamma(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2+j}x_{{}_{12}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{32}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{(-)^{j}\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over(j+n_{{}_{1}})!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(2-{D\over 2}+j)\over\Gamma({D\over 2}-1+j)}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt-{i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{D/2-2}x_{{}_{12}}^{j}x_{{}_{32}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma^{2}(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\over(j!)^{2}n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{2}})}\;.

(101)

The condition
|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>\max(|k^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;m^{2})
,

m^{2}>|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|
is essential to guarantee the multiple power series
converging. In addition,
D=4
is the first order pole of the first two triple hypergeometric
functions, and the second order pole of the third double hypergeometric
function, respectively.
Certainly the expression satisfies the system of linear PDEs
in Eq. (
53
).

Applying Horn’s study of convergence, one finds that
the absolutely and uniformly convergent region of Eq.(
101
)
is given by the domain
\Omega_{{}_{23}}^{a}
exactly.

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{D/2-1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{-1-j-n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{-1-j-n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50148pt\times{\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma({D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j)}\;.

|\xi_{{}_{33}}|(1+|x_{{}_{13}}|)<|x_{{}_{13}}x_{{}_{23}}|,\;|\xi_{{}_{33}}|(1+|x_{{}_{23}}|)<|x_{{}_{13}}x_{{}_{23}}|,\\
|\xi_{{}_{33}}|<(|x_{{}_{13}}|-|\xi_{{}_{33}}|)(|x_{{}_{23}}|-|\xi_{{}_{33}}|)\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

IV.2
The scalar integral in different convergent regions

In order to pursue our analysis, we summarize the results presented above.
The absolutely and uniformly convergent domain of
the hypergeometric function
C_{{}_{0,1}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{1}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric function
C_{{}_{0,2}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{2}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric functions
C_{{}_{0,3}}^{a}
,
C_{{}_{0,4}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{3}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric function
C_{{}_{0,6}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{6}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric function
C_{{}_{0,7}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric functions
C_{{}_{0,8}}^{a}
and
C_{{}_{0,9}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{8}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric functions
C_{{}_{0,10}}^{a}
and
C_{{}_{0,13}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{10}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric functions
C_{{}_{0,11}}^{a}
and
C_{{}_{0,14}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{11}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric functions
C_{{}_{0,18}}^{a}
and
C_{{}_{0,19}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{18}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric functions
C_{{}_{0,23}}^{a}
and
C_{{}_{0,24}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{23}}^{a}
,
that of the hypergeometric functions
C_{{}_{0,29}}^{a}
and
C_{{}_{0,30}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{29}}^{a}
,
as well as that of the hypergeometric function
C_{{}_{0,33}}^{a}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{33}}^{a}
, respectively.
The scalar integral of other parameter space contains some unknown linear combination parameters originating from
the non-isolated singularities. Among those convergent regions, the following domains
compose a set

\displaystyle\Big\{\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{a},\;\Omega_{{}_{8}}^{a},\;\Omega_{{}_{11}}^{a},\;\Omega_{{}_{18}}^{a},\;\Omega_{{}_{23}}^{a},\;\Omega_{{}_{29}}^{a}\Big\}\;,

(114)

whose element satisfies the following constraints simultaneously,

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{i}}^{a}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{j}}^{a}=\emptyset,\;i,\;j=7,\;8,\;11,\;18,\;23,\;29,\;i\neq j.

(115)

In addition, the relations between the elements of the set and those
convergent regions which do not belong to the set are

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{a}\subset\Omega_{{}_{\alpha_{{}_{1}}}}^{a},\;\alpha_{{}_{1}}=1,\;2,\;6,\;33;\;\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{a}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{1}}}}^{a}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{1}}=3,\;10;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{11}}^{a}\subset\Omega_{{}_{\alpha_{{}_{2}}}}^{a},\;\alpha_{{}_{2}}=10,\;33;\;\Omega_{{}_{11}}^{a}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{2}}}}^{a}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{2}}=1,\;2,\;3,\;6;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{29}}^{a}\subset\Omega_{{}_{10}}^{a},\;\Omega_{{}_{29}}^{a}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{3}}}}^{a}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{3}}=1,\;2,\;3,\;6,\;33;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{8}}^{a}\subset\Omega_{{}_{\alpha_{{}_{4}}}}^{a},\;\alpha_{{}_{4}}=3,\;33;\;\Omega_{{}_{8}}^{a}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{4}}}}^{a}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{4}}=1,\;2,\;6,\;10;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{23}}^{a}\subset\Omega_{{}_{3}}^{a},\;\Omega_{{}_{23}}^{a}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{5}}}}^{a}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{5}}=1,\;2,\;6,\;10,\;33;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{18}}^{a}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{6}}}}^{a}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{6}}=1,\;2,\;3,\;6,\;10,\;33.

(116)

With the preparation above the concrete expression of
C_{{}_{0}}^{a}
in different parameter space
is respectively presented as following.

\bullet
1:

In the convergent region
\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{a}
,
the scalar integral
C_{{}_{0}}^{a}
is written as the sum

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=\Big\{C_{{}_{0,1}}^{a}+C_{{}_{0,2}}^{a}+C_{{}_{0,6}}^{a}+C_{{}_{0,7}}^{a}+C_{{}_{0,33}}^{a}\Big\}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})\;.

(117)

In the limit
m^{2}\rightarrow 0
,
the expression of Eq.(
117
) recovers that presented in Eq.(
35
).
Furthermore, we expand the hypergeometric functions
C_{{}_{0,i}}^{a},\;(i=1,\;2,\;6,\;7,\;33)

around
\varepsilon=0
, and find that
D=4
is the pole of the second order for
each hypergeometric function individually. Nevertheless the sum presented in
Eq.(
117
) is an analytic function of the dimension in the
neighborhood of
D=4
because those singularities are canceled clearly,
and the final result is given as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}^{a}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})={i(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Big\{f_{{}_{\varepsilon}}(x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+2\varepsilon\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}A_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{\prime(a)}(x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(j)\Gamma^{2}(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+2\varepsilon\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}B_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(a)}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(j)\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma^{2}(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+2\varepsilon\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{31}}^{j}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}C_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(a)}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(j)\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma^{2}(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\Big({\xi_{{}_{33}}\over x_{{}_{13}}x_{{}_{23}}}\Big)^{j}\xi_{{}_{31}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma^{2}(j)\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big[\psi(j+n_{{}_{1}})+\psi(j+n_{{}_{2}})-2\psi(j)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-{1\over j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}+\ln{\xi_{{}_{33}}\over x_{{}_{13}}x_{{}_{23}}}+{\varepsilon\over 2}D_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(a)}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+2\varepsilon\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{31}}^{j}\xi_{{}_{32}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}\xi_{{}_{33}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{(-)^{j+n_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma(j)\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma^{2}(1+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{2})\Big\}\;.

(118)

with

\displaystyle f_{{}_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)=\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}x^{n_{{}_{1}}}y^{n_{{}_{2}}}{\Gamma^{2}(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\Big[\ln x\ln y-2\ln x\psi(1+n_{{}_{2}})-2\ln y\psi(1+n_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+2\ln(xy)\psi(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})+4\psi(1+n_{{}_{1}})\psi(1+n_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-4\{\psi(1+n_{{}_{1}})+\psi(1+n_{{}_{2}})\}\psi(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+4\psi^{2}(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})+2\psi^{(1)}(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})-{\varepsilon\over 2}A_{{}_{n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(a)}(x,y)\Big]\;.

(119)

Here the coefficients of
\varepsilon
above
A_{{}_{n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(a)}(x,y)

V
The
C_{{}_{0}}
function with three equal masses

In this case, the Mellin-Barnes representation of the scalar integral is

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}^{b}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=\int{d^{D}q\over(2\pi)^{D}}{1\over[q^{2}-m^{2}][(q+p_{{}_{1}})^{2}-m^{2}][(q-p_{{}_{2}})^{2}-m^{2}]}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt=-{i(-)^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}(2\pi i)^{3}}\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}ds(-m^{2})^{s}\Gamma(-s)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}dz_{{}_{1}}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2})^{z_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})\int_{-i\infty}^{+i\infty}dz_{{}_{2}}(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{z_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3-s-z_{{}_{1}}-z_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+s+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s-z_{{}_{2}})\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s-z_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(1+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(D-3-2s)}\;,

(140)

which is equivalent to the representation of Eq. (36) of Ref.
Davydychev3
,
and that of Eq. (4.5) of Ref.
Davydychev1991JMP
.
Applying the residue theorem to the singularities
of
\Gamma
functions in the numerator of integrand, we formulate the Mellin-Barnes representation
as various hypergeometric functions whose absolutely and uniformly
convergent regions are analyzed through Horn’s study of convergence
Horn1889
.
As the scalar integral is given by

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}^{b}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})={i(-)^{D/2}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}F_{{}_{b,k}}(\xi_{{}_{m3}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}})\;,

(141)

with
\xi_{{}_{mj}}=\xi_{{}_{ij}},\;(i,\;j=1,\;2,\;3)
. In addition,
the dimensionless function
F_{{}_{b,k}}
satisfies the
system of linear PDEs

\displaystyle\Big\{(3-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{m3}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{i3}}})(5-D+2\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{m3}}})(4-D+2\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{m3}}})

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-{1\over\xi_{{}_{m3}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{m3}}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{2}(2-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{m3}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{i3}}})\Big\}F_{{}_{b,p_{{}_{3}}}}=0\;,

\displaystyle\Big\{(3-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{m3}}}+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{i3}}})(1+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{2}\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{i3}}})

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-{1\over x_{{}_{j3}}}\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{j3}}}(2-{D\over 2}+\hat{\vartheta}_{\xi_{{}_{m3}}}+\hat{\vartheta}_{x_{{}_{j3}}})\Big\}F_{{}_{b,p_{{}_{3}}}}=0\;,\;(j=1,\;2)\;.

(142)

Now we present our results below in detail.

V.1
The hypergeometric functions and their convergent regions

\bullet
1:

For the isolated singularities defined by the poles of

\Gamma(-s)
,
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
, and
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})
, the
corresponding hypergeometric series is written as:

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,1}}^{b}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=-{i(-)^{D/2-3}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}{\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+n_{{}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-{i(-)^{D/2-3}(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}{\Gamma^{2}(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma^{2}({D\over 2}-1)\over\Gamma(D-3)\Gamma(4-D)}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(4-D+2j)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}})}\;.

(143)

Here the condition
|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>\max(m^{2},\;|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|)

should be satisfied to guarantee the convergence of hypergeometric function.
In addition,
D=4
is the second order pole of the first double hypergeometric
function, and the first order pole of the second triple hypergeometric
function, respectively. Certainly the expression
of Eq. (
143
) complies with the system of linear PDEs in Eq. (
142
).

The absolutely and uniformly convergent region of the hypergeometric function is

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{1}}^{b}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{m3}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\xi_{{}_{m3}}|<1/4,\\
\sqrt{|x_{{}_{13}}|}+\sqrt{|x_{{}_{23}}|}<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

\bullet
2:

When the isolated singularities are defined by the poles of

\Gamma(-s)
,
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
, and
\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s-z_{{}_{2}})
,
the derived hypergeometric series
C_{{}_{0,2}}^{b}
is given by Eq. (
240
), where
the necessary condition
|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>\max(m^{2},\;|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|)
,
|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>m^{2}

should be met to guarantee the hypergeometric series converging.
By implementing the residue theorem to the Mellin-Barnes representation, one derives the first expression.
The second expression decomposes the summation indices of the hypergeometric series
in the original expression, so
D=4
is the pole of the same fixed order
for each term of every hypergeometric series of Eq. (
240
).
Furthermore, the decomposition of the summation indices is correct and reasonable
because the second expression also satisfies the system of linear PDEs
in Eq. (
142
).
Horn’s study of convergence predicts the absolutely and uniformly convergent region of the
double hypergeometric functions as

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{2}}^{b}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{m3}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\xi_{{}_{m3}}x_{{}_{13}}|<|x_{{}_{23}}|,\;|\xi_{{}_{m3}}|<|x_{{}_{23}}|/4,\\
\sqrt{|x_{{}_{13}}|}+\sqrt{|x_{{}_{23}}|}<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

\bullet
3:

For the isolated singularities defined by the poles of

\Gamma(-s)
,
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
, and
\Gamma(1+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
,
the corresponding hypergeometric series
C_{{}_{0,3}}^{b}
is

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,3}}^{b}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=C_{{}_{0,2}}^{b}(p_{{}_{3}}\rightarrow p_{{}_{2}},\;\xi_{{}_{m3}}\rightarrow\xi_{{}_{m2}},\;x_{{}_{13}}\rightarrow x_{{}_{12}},\;x_{{}_{23}}\rightarrow x_{{}_{32}})\;,

(150)

where the condition
|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>\max(m^{2},\;|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|)
,
|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>m^{2}

is necessary to guarantee the convergence of the hypergeometric functions.
The absolutely and uniformly convergent region of Eq.(
150
) is similarly given as

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{3}}^{b}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{m2}},\;x_{{}_{12}},\;x_{{}_{32}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\xi_{{}_{m2}}x_{{}_{12}}|<|x_{{}_{32}}|,\;|\xi_{{}_{m2}}|<|x_{{}_{32}}|/4,\\
\sqrt{|x_{{}_{12}}|}+\sqrt{|x_{{}_{32}}|}<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

\Gamma(-s)
,

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,4}}^{b}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=C_{{}_{0,1}}^{b}(p_{{}_{3}}\rightarrow p_{{}_{2}},\;\xi_{{}_{m3}}\rightarrow\xi_{{}_{m2}},\;x_{{}_{13}}\rightarrow x_{{}_{12}},\;x_{{}_{23}}\rightarrow x_{{}_{32}})\;.

(154)

The condition
|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>\max(m^{2},\;|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|)

is necessary to guarantee the hypergeometric functions converging.
In addition, the absolutely and uniformly convergent region of the term is

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{4}}^{b}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{m2}},\;x_{{}_{12}},\;x_{{}_{32}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\xi_{{}_{m2}}|<1/4,\\
\sqrt{|x_{{}_{12}}|}+\sqrt{|x_{{}_{32}}|}<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

\bullet
6:

When the isolated singularities are produced by the poles of

\Gamma(-s)
,
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})
, and
\Gamma({D\over 2}-2-s-z_{{}_{1}})
,
the corresponding hypergeometric series
C_{{}_{0,6}}^{b}
is presented in Eq. (
241
), where
the condition
|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>\max(m^{2},\;|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|)
,
|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|>m^{2}

should be satisfied to guarantee the convergence of the hypergeometric functions.
Applying the residue theorem to the Mellin-Barnes representation, one derives the first expression of Eq. (
241
).
The second expression decomposes the summation indices of the hypergeometric series
in the original expression, so
D=4
is the pole of the same fixed order
for each term of every hypergeometric series of the second equation in Eq. (
241
).
In addition, the decomposition of the summation indices is correct and reasonable
because the second expression also complies with the system of linear PDEs
in Eq. (
142
).

The concretely convergent region of the hypergeometric functions is given by

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{6}}^{b}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{m3}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\xi_{{}_{m3}}x_{{}_{23}}|<|x_{{}_{13}}|,\;|\xi_{{}_{m3}}|<|x_{{}_{13}}|/4,\\
\sqrt{|x_{{}_{13}}|}+\sqrt{|x_{{}_{23}}|}<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

7:

\Gamma(-s)
,

, and

,

|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|m^{2}

is essential to guarantee the convergence of the hypergeometric functions.
Applying the residue theorem to the Mellin-Barnes representation, one derives the first expression of Eq. (
242
).
The second expression decomposes the summation indices of the hypergeometric series
in the original expression, so
D=4
is the pole of the same fixed order
for each term of every hypergeometric series of the second equation in Eq. (
242
).
In addition, the decomposition of the summation indices is correct and reasonable
because the subsequent expression also satisfies the system of linear PDEs
in Eq. (
142
).

Correspondingly the absolutely and uniformly convergent region of
this hypergeometric function is concretely written as

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{b}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{m3}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\xi_{{}_{m3}}x_{{}_{13}}|<|x_{{}_{23}}|,\;|\xi_{{}_{m3}}x_{{}_{23}}|<|x_{{}_{13}}|,\\

|\xi_{{}_{m3}}|<|x_{{}_{23}}|/4,\;\sqrt{|x_{{}_{13}}|}+\sqrt{|x_{{}_{23}}|}<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

, the

|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>\max(|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|,\;m^{2})
,
m^{2}>|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|

should be satisfied to guarantee the hypergeometric series converging.

,
m^{2}>|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|

\bullet
19:

For the isolated singularities defined by the poles of

\Gamma(-z_{{}_{1}})
,
\Gamma(-z_{{}_{2}})
, and
\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+s+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
,
the corresponding hypergeometric series is given as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,19}}^{b}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=-{i(m^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\eta_{{}_{3m}}^{j}\eta_{{}_{1m}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}\eta_{{}_{2m}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(3+2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})}\;.

(190)

The necessary condition
4m^{2}>\max(|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|)

should be met to guarantee the convergence of the triple series.
In addition,
D=4
is the first order pole of the hypergeometric
function. Certainly the expression
of Eq. (
190
) complies with the system of linear PDEs in Eq. (
142
).

Correspondingly the convergent region of the term is

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{19}}^{b}=\left\{(\eta_{{}_{1m}},\;\eta_{{}_{2m}},\;\eta_{{}_{3m}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\eta_{{}_{1m}}|<4,\;|\eta_{{}_{2m}}|<4,\;|\eta_{{}_{3m}}|<4\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

20:

,

,

,

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{-n_{{}_{1}}-n_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(2+2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma({D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(2+j+2n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})}

|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|m^{2}
,
|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|>|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|m^{2}

|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|m^{2}>|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|
should be satisfied to guarantee
the convergence of the hypergeometric series.

\bullet
33:

When the isolated singularities are produced by the poles of

\Gamma(D/2-2-s-z_{{}_{1}})
,
\Gamma(D/2-2-s-z_{{}_{2}})
, and
\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+s+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
,
the corresponding hypergeometric series is given as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0,33}}^{b}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})={i(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Big\{\Gamma(2-{D\over 2})\Gamma({D\over 2}-1)\Big\}{\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{D/2-1}\over x_{{}_{13}}x_{{}_{23}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{-j-n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{-j-n_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{(-)^{j}\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(2+2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma({D\over 2}+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(2+2j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})}\;.

(199)

The necessary condition
\min(|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|)>m^{2}
,

|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|m^{2}
should be met to guarantee
the convergence of the hypergeometric series.
In addition,
D=4
is the first order pole of the hypergeometric
function. Certainly the expression of Eq. (
199
) complies with
the system of linear PDEs in Eq. (
142
).

The convergent region is apparently written as

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{33}}^{b}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{m3}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\xi_{{}_{m3}}|<|x_{{}_{13}}x_{{}_{23}}|,\;|\xi_{{}_{m3}}|<|x_{{}_{13}}|/4,\;|\xi_{{}_{m3}}|<|x_{{}_{23}}|/4\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

\bullet
34:

For the singularities defined by the poles of
\Gamma(D/2-2-s-z_{{}_{1}})
,

\Gamma(1+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
, and
\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+s+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
,
the scalar integral contains some undefined linear combination parameters
originating from the non-isolated singularities.
The necessary condition
|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>\max(|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|,\;m^{2})
,

|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|m^{2}
,
|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|>m^{2}
should be satisfied to guarantee
the convergence of the hypergeometric series.

\bullet
35:

For the singularities defined by the poles of
\Gamma(D/2-2-s-z_{{}_{2}})
,

\Gamma(1+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
, and
\Gamma(3-{D\over 2}+s+z_{{}_{1}}+z_{{}_{2}})
,
the scalar integral contains some undefined linear combination parameters
originating from the non-isolated singularities.
The necessary condition
|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}|>\max(|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|,\;|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|,\;m^{2})
,

|p_{{}_{1}}^{2}p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>|p_{{}_{3}}^{2}|m^{2}
,
|p_{{}_{2}}^{2}|>m^{2}
should be satisfied to guarantee
the convergence of the hypergeometric series.

V.2
The scalar integral in different convergent regions

The absolutely and uniformly convergent region of
the hypergeometric function
C_{{}_{0,\alpha}}
is the domain
\Omega_{{}_{\alpha}}^{b}
,

\alpha=1,\cdots,\;4,\;6,\;\cdots,\;11,\;13,\;14,\;19,\;21,\;27,\;33
.
The scalar integral of other parameter space contains some unknown linear combination parameters originating from
the non-isolated singularities. Among those convergent regions, the following domains
compose a set

\displaystyle\Big\{\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{b},\;\Omega_{{}_{8}}^{b},\;\Omega_{{}_{11}}^{b},\;\Omega_{{}_{19}}^{b}\Big\}\;,

(202)

whose element satisfies the following constraints simultaneously,

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{i}}^{b}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{j}}^{b}=\emptyset,\;i,\;j=7,\;8,\;11,\;19,\;i\neq j.

(203)

Meanwhile, the relations between the elements of the set and those
convergent regions which do not belong to the set are

\displaystyle\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{b}\subset\Omega_{{}_{\alpha_{{}_{1}}}}^{b},\;\alpha_{{}_{1}}=1,\;2,\;6,\;7,\;21,\;27,\;33;\;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{b}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{1}}}}^{b}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{1}}=3,\;4,\;8,\;9,\;10,\;11,\;13,\;14,\;19;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{8}}^{b}\subset\Omega_{{}_{\alpha_{{}_{2}}}}^{b},\;\alpha_{{}_{2}}=3,\;4,\;8,\;9,\;21,\;27,\;33;\;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{8}}^{b}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{2}}}}^{b}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{2}}=1,\;2,\;6,\;7,\;10,\;11,\;13,\;14,\;19;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{11}}^{b}\subset\Omega_{{}_{\alpha_{{}_{3}}}}^{b},\;\alpha_{{}_{3}}=10,\;11,\;13,\;14,\;21,\;27,\;33;\;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{11}}^{b}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{3}}}}^{b}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{3}}=1,\;2,\;3,\;4,\;6,\;7,\;8,\;9,\;19;

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Omega_{{}_{19}}^{b}\bigcap\Omega_{{}_{\beta_{{}_{4}}}}^{b}=\emptyset,\;\beta_{{}_{4}}=1,\cdots,\;4,\;6,\cdots,\;11,\;13,\;14,\;21,\;27,\;33.

(204)

With the preparation above the concrete expression of
C_{{}_{0}}
in different parameter space
is respectively presented as following.

\bullet
1:

In the convergent region
\Omega_{{}_{7}}^{b}
,
the scalar integral
C_{{}_{0}}^{b}
is written as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}^{b}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})=\Big\{C_{{}_{0,1}}^{b}+C_{{}_{0,2}}^{b}+C_{{}_{0,6}}^{b}+C_{{}_{0,7}}^{b}+C_{{}_{0,21}}^{b}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+C_{{}_{0,27}}^{b}+C_{{}_{0,33}}^{b}\Big\}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})

(205)

In the limit
m^{2}\rightarrow 0
, the expression of Eq.(
205
)
recovers that presented in Eq.(
35
).
Furthermore,
D=4
is the pole of the second order for
each hypergeometric function individually in the above expression. Nevertheless the scalar integral presented in
Eq.(
205
) is an analytic function of the dimension in the
neighborhood of
D=4
because those singularities are canceled clearly,
and the final result is given as

\displaystyle C_{{}_{0}}^{b}(p_{{}_{1}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{2}}^{2},\;p_{{}_{3}}^{2},\;m^{2})={i(p_{{}_{3}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}\Big\{f_{{}_{\varepsilon}}(x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+2\varepsilon\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{j}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}A_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(b)}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(2j)\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{-j-n_{{}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{1}})\over(j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(1+j+2n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(j)}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big[\Big(-2\psi(2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})-\psi(j+n_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\psi(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})+2\psi(1+j+2n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})+\psi(1+n_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\psi(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})-\ln{x_{{}_{13}}\xi_{{}_{m3}}\over x_{{}_{23}}}\Big)+{\varepsilon\over 2}B_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(b)}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{-j-n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{(-)^{n_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{2}})\over(j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})!n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(j)}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big[\Big(-2\psi(2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})-\psi(j+n_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\psi(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})+2\psi(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})+\psi(1+n_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\psi(1+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})-\ln{x_{{}_{23}}\xi_{{}_{m3}}\over x_{{}_{13}}}\Big)+{\varepsilon\over 2}C_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(b)}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{-j-n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{-j-n_{{}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{(-)^{j}\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})\over\Gamma(j)n_{{}_{1}}!n_{{}_{2}}!\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(2j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big[\Big(2\psi(2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})-2\psi(2j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})-\psi(j)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\psi(j+n_{{}_{1}})+\psi(j+n_{{}_{2}})-\psi(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\ln{x_{{}_{13}}x_{{}_{23}}\over\xi_{{}_{m3}}}\Big)+{\varepsilon\over 2}D_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(b)}\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=1}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{-j}x_{{}_{23}}^{-n_{{}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(j)\Gamma(n_{{}_{1}})\over(j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})!(n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})!(j+n_{{}_{2}})!\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big[1+\varepsilon\Big(2\psi(2j+2n_{{}_{1}}+2n_{{}_{2}})-2\psi(j+n_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\psi(1+n_{{}_{2}})+\psi(j)+\psi(n_{{}_{1}})-\ln(x_{{}_{13}}x_{{}_{23}})\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-2\varepsilon\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=0}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m}}^{j+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{-j}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(2j+2n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(j)\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{1}})\over(2j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})!(j+n_{{}_{1}})!(j+n_{{}_{2}})!n_{{}_{2}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big(\ln x_{{}_{13}}-\psi(1+2j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})-\psi(1+j+n_{{}_{1}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\psi(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})+\psi(1+n_{{}_{2}})\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-2\varepsilon\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=1}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m}}^{j+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{-j}x_{{}_{23}}^{j+n_{{}_{2}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times{\Gamma(2j+2n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(j)\Gamma(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(1+n_{{}_{2}})\over(2j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})!(j+n_{{}_{1}})!(j+n_{{}_{2}})!n_{{}_{1}}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big(\ln x_{{}_{23}}-\psi(1+2j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})-\psi(1+j+n_{{}_{2}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\psi(1+j+n_{{}_{1}}+n_{{}_{2}})+\psi(1+n_{{}_{1}})\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+2\varepsilon\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=1}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{-n_{{}_{1}}}{(-)^{n_{{}_{1}}}\Gamma(2j+2n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(n_{{}_{1}})\over n_{{}_{1}}!j!\Gamma(1+j+2n_{{}_{1}})}E_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}}}}^{(b)}(x_{{}_{13}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+2\varepsilon\sum\limits_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{2}}=1}^{\infty}\xi_{{}_{m3}}^{j+n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{13}}^{-n_{{}_{2}}}x_{{}_{23}}^{n_{{}_{2}}}{(-)^{n_{{}_{2}}}\Gamma(2j+2n_{{}_{2}})\Gamma(n_{{}_{2}})\over n_{{}_{2}}!j!\Gamma(1+j+2n_{{}_{2}})}E_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(b)}(x_{{}_{23}})

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{n_{{}_{1}}=0}^{\infty}x_{{}_{m}}^{j+n_{{}_{1}}}x_{{}_{1}}^{-j}x_{{}_{2}}^{-n_{{}_{1}}}{\Gamma(2j+2n_{{}_{1}})\Gamma(j)\Gamma(n_{{}_{1}})\over j!n_{{}_{1}}!(j+n_{{}_{1}})!\Gamma(j+n_{{}_{1}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\Big[1+G_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}}}}^{(b)}(x_{{}_{13}}x_{{}_{23}})\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{2})\Big\}\;,

(206)

where the coefficients of
\varepsilon
above
A_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(b)}
,
B_{{}_{j,n_{{}_{1}},n_{{}_{2}}}}^{(b)}
,

and

can be found in Eq.(

The absolutely and uniformly converging region

is a proper subset of the cube

\displaystyle\Xi_{{}_{\rm K}}^{b}=\left\{(\xi_{{}_{m3}},\;x_{{}_{13}},\;x_{{}_{23}}):\left|\begin{array}[]{l}|\xi_{{}_{m3}}|<1,\;|x_{{}_{13}}|<1,\;|x_{{}_{23}}|<1\end{array}\right.\right\}\;.

from the region

) with the finite element method. We will consider this

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt={i(p_{{}_{2}}^{2})^{D/2-3}\over(4\pi)^{D/2}}F_{{}_{b,p_{{}_{2}}}}(\xi_{{}_{m2}},\;x_{{}_{12}},\;x_{{}_{32}})

,

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+{\cal O}(\varepsilon^{2})\Big]\;.

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-{1\over y_{{}_{im}}}\hat{\theta}_{y_{{}_{im}}}\Big(1+2\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\hat{\theta}_{\eta_{{}_{jm}}}\Big)\Big(2+2\sum\limits_{j=1}^{3}\hat{\theta}_{\eta_{{}_{jm}}}\Big)\Big\}F_{{}_{b,m}}=0,\;(i=1,\;2,\;3)\;.

is a proper subset of the absolutely and uniformly converging region

.

C_{{}_{0}}^{b}
from the region
\Omega_{{}_{19}}^{b}

to the relevant parameter space, one employs the system of linear PDEs in Eq. (
